ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u3	Thank you very much. We proceed with the examination of items 42 and 43 of the agenda: 42. First reading of the government draft Public Procurement Law (Print No 3624). 43. First reading of the government draft law Provisions introducing the Public Procurement Law (Print No 3625). I ask the Minister of Entrepreneurship and Technology Jadwiga Emilewicz to provide the justification for the draft laws contained in prints 3624 and 3625.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u4	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, High Izbo! I have the honour and pleasure to present to you a draft Public Procurement Law which regulates the participation of the public sector in the Polish economy, as well as the provisions introducing this law into the legal system. I feel that this is a very important act, one of the most important that will affect the way and quality of doing business in Poland. She introduces new rules of the game and why she is so important and so reported, I will try to introduce you. The current law, which was established in 2004, and thus just before Poland entered the European Union, before the huge transfer of public funds, no longer meets its requirements, does not introduce rules which are important, so attractive to the private sector that it would like, to the extent possible, to benefit from this market. We are convinced that public procurement - I think that everyone who has ever had local but also business experience - is also convinced of this - is a powerful tool supporting economic development. They help improve the quality of supplies of services, works, they can serve - and we want them to support - innovation, but also the implementation of social policies and state development strategies. As part of them, he orders over 34 thousand goods, services and works in Poland. entities and the value of contracts awarded last year reached almost 10% Polish GDP. Last year it was over 202 billion PLN, and this value, as we see, year to year, has grown in recent years. In the hands of the Polish state, but also in the hands of the Polish market there is no second such large stream of funds, which year by year reach the market. Properly prepared procedures corresponding to the real situation can and should be one of the main sources of income and an incentive to develop investments for Polish companies. In recent years, expectations of the way public funds have been spent have changed significantly. We are becoming less and less interested in only the price, and we attach more and more importance to the quality, quantity, technological advancement of the goods and services provided. At the same time, the Community's European policy, which we are members of, is making us increasingly demanding that services and goods be sustainable in order to be environmentally friendly. This is also, of course, another challenge for entrepreneurs, but above all for those who order the services and goods in question. On the other hand, given the pool of available resources, it is extremely important that public procurement pursues the strategic objectives that we set ourselves, the development objectives for the whole state. One example, of the elements that we are trying to develop and ensure in the new public procurement law, is that those who win the proceedings should, on fair, fair terms, employ the contractors who provide the services in question, therefore the award of employees employed for the contract has been clearly and unambiguously recorded in this new law. We also include certain specific social aspects in public procurement through the reservation of certain types of procurement, e.g. for disabled persons. By moving away from the price criterion, we take into account the environmental and economic issues I mentioned earlier. I think it is also important that - because the bill is very large and we realise at which point in time we are working - we strongly believe that it is a very well prepared and developed project. Work on it started more than 3 years ago and we started with extensive consultations: with contracting authorities, with local authorities, with all the entities that implement public procurement in Poland, with entities that have joined these contracts in recent years and which have addressed, reported many problems, many inability, but also with entities that have not used these contracts, in order to gain knowledge why this market was not attractive for them. We have looked at all the doubts and demands that have been sent to us, and we have sorted out the requests that have been made during many - more than 100 - consultations, discussions and meetings that we have had during these 3 years. One of the fundamental problems that we have identified from the perspective of the public sector is the deteriorating level of competitiveness of public procurement from over 10 years onwards. In the year 2004 mentioned by me, when the current law entered into force, we had between five and seven applicants in one proceeding. Last year these entities were statistically few more than two. And there's a pool of orders, over 50%. those contracts to which only one entity joins. I have repeatedly pointed out that one such sector, where we have very many companies, is the IT sector, and orders are particularly important because they can improve the quality of public services in an excellent way. For these orders last year just over 50% such contracts were only dealt with by one tenderer. What the consequences are, I don't have to talk here for sure. This means that, first of all, there are many companies that do not benefit from such a development stimulus, even though they provide services that could meet the expectations of the contracting authority. But this is also, of course, an impact on prices, because we know, we are aware, and any analysis shows that the more operators apply for the procedure, the more costs can ultimately be significantly lower. In the case of our statistics, we certainly see a huge area to improve efficiency, to reduce the costs of functioning of the public administration precisely by the fact that these applicants will simply be more. Based on these results, we prepared the concept of the new Public Procurement Law in the first place in spring last year. This was the first document to be consulted. It is on the basis of this document that we later wrote specific provisions that we are sending to you today for further work. This broad consultation, as I said, concerned first the development of the concept itself and later the laws themselves. I don't think it's going to be an exaggeration if I say there were really hundreds of people involved. We met, we held conferences in nine cities in Poland, they were large conferences, but there were hundreds of industry meetings, individual meetings. I think it's important, too, that we've taken experience from the market. We are aware that large private actors today also have very restrictive procurement regimes, precisely to improve the efficiency of their activities, so we have taken from the market a lot of the experience that today is becoming part of our law. Based on this collected material, we started work on a government bill. The project was ready at the beginning of this year. We've started a normal government procedure. Interministerial consultations were held, public consultations and a new project was adopted by the Council of Ministers in June this year. What is very important, I think, in this whole cycle of work on the project - we also sought the opinions of the Joint Government and Local Government Commission, the Social Dialogue Council and trade union organisations, as well as outstanding representatives of the world of science, including. The Legislative Board of the Prime Minister. All of these organizations and all of these bodies spoke positively about our project. I emphasize it here because it's a bill that... In a natural way, these are actors - representatives of local government on the one hand, on the other hand employers - who rarely have a common view, a good view of the proposed legislative changes. In this case, we have managed to reconcile these seemingly conflicting interests. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The main objective pursued by the new Public Procurement Law project is to increase the efficiency of the public procurement system mentioned by me. We realize what efficiency is. This is getting the best possible results in the shortest time at the best possible price. These effects can, of course, be different. The result may be the fastest delivery of office equipment, but it may also be the construction of a building in such a technology that will make its long-term operation much cheaper than other available technology. But another effect is also to increase the potential of small and medium-sized companies. Finally, the result is also very expected by us to increase the level of innovation, and therefore a new, more attractive, more interesting way of doing its tasks, on the one hand, by the municipality, e.g. in the field of waste management. This is an extremely important innovation reserve right now. In order to achieve this goal, the new law has the task of creating a public procurement system that will respond to all the most important problems of the market. Every problem that we have identified as part of an open consultation first with a concept, and then with Public Procurement Law, we answer, we hope, we are convinced, with concrete solutions. The first one I mentioned here is the disproportionate share of small and medium-sized companies in the public procurement market. We always remind you that small and medium-sized companies are more than 90% entities active in the Polish economic landscape and the public procurement market definitely does not reflect this landscape. So we've identified barriers, and we've tried to measure them. How do we do that? First of all, for national proceedings below the EU thresholds, we are introducing a new, easier procedure that does not require procedures based on EU directives. Operators will be able to award contracts using a flexible, simplified procedure, which provides the possibility, in particular, of negotiating tenders to a wider or narrower extent, depending on the contracting authority's decision. This is a special procedure for this type of order. We hope that this will encourage more players, small and medium-sized, to participate in this market. At the same time, we are aware that small and medium-sized companies are very often taking part in the public procurement market as subcontractors. So, by introducing solutions that are beneficial for contractors, we have also not forgotten about subcontractors. We also want to ensure that they enjoy all the rights that contractors enjoy. We bind contractors to care for the interests of subcontractors in an appropriate way. Therefore, among others. the Act contains a provision which ensures that the provisions of contracts with subcontractors will not be less favourable than those of contracts with contractors in terms of contractual penalties, payments or valorisation. Opening up to SMEs will also serve to remodel the competition procedure. The fact that this is a good tool has been experienced by the implementation of the GovTech programme initiated by Mr Moravian Prime Minister 2 years ago. Many such competition procedures, especially in the field of IT services for administrations, have been effectively carried out. Using these experiences, we have definitely simplified the competition procedures. The new competition will allow, among others. for the conclusion of a contract for the performance of a service with which competition work was involved and not only for the development of a project carried out. This is the short access path, i.e. the winner of the competition will not have to proceed to a complex, complex procedure, only on the basis of the winning competition you will be able to sign with him a contract for the execution of the entire order. We are convinced that this element will definitely open up the public procurement market for innovative companies, for a sector that is definitely not taking part in public procurement today. Changes in this respect to facilitate small and medium-sized enterprises also required a reference to the issue of the security of the proper performance of the contract. Today, liquidity problems very often make small companies unable to participate in tenders, because they simply cannot afford a security deposit or security. These requirements, which are today, as all actors have reported, are far too excessive. Therefore, in smaller national simplified procedures, the maximum value of the security which the contracting authority may require is reduced by half, i.e. up to 1.5%. At the same time, the project assumes that the security will be optional in all proceedings, i.e. that the ordering party will be able to, but he won't have to expect it. [[♪ The bell ♪]] In addition, the maximum security that the contracting authority may require shall be 10%. the total price quoted in the tender. In practice, many contracting authorities require a security of this kind, although not always such a security is justified by the specific nature of the contract.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u5	Thank you very much, Minister. Minister of Enterprise and Technology Jadwiga Emilewicz: These solutions are, of course, much more, not just for the small and medium-sized enterprises sector. It is also a huge facilitation for the public sector. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I recommend that you accept the new Public Procurement Law. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u6	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft of the new Public Procurement Law presented by the Minister of Entrepreneurship and Technology is a response to the voices of the whole public procurement market, which has been waiting for the law for several years, which will respond to the needs of today's economic situation and the problems of spending a huge stream of public money. The new Public Procurement Law creates a completely new framework for more efficient public spending, focused primarily on successful performance of the contract between the trader and the public, rather than on the formal side of the procedure. Project of this objective in the form of the effectiveness is achieved by focusing on the neglected stages of the procurement procedure, planning, preparation and subsequent implementation of the performance evaluation of the contract in the current act. Today, public entities focus primarily on the selection of the contractor, sometimes not paying sufficient attention to other elements of the whole process, which are equally important and sometimes even more important, because a successful contract can only be spoken of when the entity of the contract is fully executed. Before I move on to these specific amendments, which the law contains very much, I would like to mention that it is important that the law retains some important achievements introduced in the system by the 2016 amendment to the Public Procurement Law. One of them is to continue to depart from the price dictation as the only criterion for selecting offers for quality in particular. The second very important topic is the so-called question. in-house contracts, i.e. tasks contracted to entities dependent on the contracting authority, e.g. municipal companies. However, some clarification is introduced to ensure that this institution is not abused. It is worth mentioning at least a few of the solutions contained in the Law addressing problems mainly related to low competitiveness in this market or too little participation in the SME sector. The introduction of state purchasing policy, focusing purchases on innovation, sustainable products and services provided to public bodies. Purchasing policy is intended to be a coherent and strategic spending plan. Introduce the principle of efficiency in the award of public contracts, i.e. the highest possible economic or social benefits from the funds spent with the best possible ratio of expenditure to the benefits obtained. Introduce an obligation to carry out an analysis prior to the initiation of a public procurement procedure, in particular a high value contract, both on the procedural side and on the economic and technical side. Simplifying the use of procedures suitable for the award of public contracts. Negotiations pave the way for contracting to gain knowledge of the best innovative solutions that contractors can offer. Introduction of the principle of cooperation between the contracting authority and the contractor at the stage of the performance of the contract. The proposal implies expressing the obligation of the contracting authority and the contractor to cooperate directly in order to ensure the proper performance of the contract. Separation of a simplified procedure for contracts below the Union thresholds. Proposals in the project that respond directly to the problem of low competitiveness include:: simplifications in the qualifications of economic operators, including limitation of exclusion conditions; obligation to apply advances or partial payments in longer contracts; mandatory valorisation in contracts concluded for more than 12 months or a catalogue of prohibited clauses. In addition, a settlement mechanism was provided for to settle the biggest disputes between the contractor and the contracting authority at the stage of the performance of the contract. Important changes also concern the system of legal protection measures, namely the reduction of the charge on the complaint against the case law of the KIO, the extension of the time limit for bringing an action against the judgment of the KIO. The procurement control system will be developed through common general rules on the control of public procurement, on the basis of an analysis of the risk of an infringement or on the obligation to publish guidelines for control by control authorities. The role of the President of the Public Procurement Office in preparing and promoting good practice and model documents will also be strengthened, [[♪ The bell ♪]] which will constitute...

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u8	...support for the contracting authority.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u46	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! First of all, I thank you very much for all these questions, and I think we agreed on one thing, that this is a law very much needed. I understand the objections, and maybe I'll talk to them first because they've come up with a lot of questions. It is about reservations about the time of the bill and the size of the bill. Firstly, once again, I stress that we have made every effort to make it a well-prepared project so that in the works of the parliament the state does not have to write a de facto law again, only so that we can get to know it well and possibly make cosmetic corrections. Secondly, today we are aware, and you know that, as a reminder of the S1 we have in public procurement. Therefore, my question is: Are you ready today to entrust entrepreneurs, small and medium-sized subcontractors, large ones who come down from buildings that do not want to implement them, who do not have the possibility to move to the conciliation mode, but also to the contracting system, but also to citizens who will not have the road to be waiting for the next years with this bill? This law gives us the opportunity to accelerate the many construction works that we are doing today. Recognizing the importance of the act and the time we are in, we also decided at the beginning of this year to introduce a long vacathio legis, so that, first of all, there is no doubt that the ordering party will have 3 months to learn the new rules. We therefore assumed that the date of entry into force of this law would be 1 January 2021. Then, if there are any doubts in further analysis, the new term of office will be able to make any changes, which I hope will not happen, because this time, now, until the end of this term, will allow this well-prepared project to be finally refined. As far as the length of this bill is concerned, of course, we can count this because of meters or numbers. Indeed, the present law has slightly more than 360 articles, and this is bigger. But if you, especially the members of the economic committee, are well aware of the current Public Procurement Law, then you know that there are many articles in which points from a to z are included under one number, so if we were to calculate the real weight, then this weight would be the same. You are aware that we are implementing complex, complex European law. We have tried in this law to apply the principle which we apply at all in the Ministry of Entrepreneurship and Technology, i.e. not to embezzle law, not to overwrite what Community law requires of us. Many questions concerned small and medium-sized companies, facilitating them. I mentioned that this was a separation of the rules related to orders to the so-called. The EU thresholds make the smallest ones not have to stray from Article. 1 to Article 640, they only have their own separate part. This easier procedure is also an element addressed to them. As far as these architects are concerned - small and medium-sized companies - this is the competition procedure that allows us to sign the contract after the competition. This solution is addressed to this group in particular. If we are talking about small and medium-sized companies, we are also thinking with particular care about improving their liquidity, hence also reducing the requirements for deposits and advances, as well as the obligation to pay advances and advance payments when we have an agreement that goes beyond a period of 12 months. Now the question concerning the local government units and the number of tenders that you have mentioned until today has not been settled. In the new rules we assume that even if the selected contractor withdraws from the performance of the contract, the contracting authority will be able to choose the second in turn, even during the duration of the contract. We have assumed, we hope, a situation where we have one approacher and a non-fulfilling failure to comply with the agreement. That's how we try to address it. Also simplifying the procedure. What is most important to us is an increase in the number of applicants for a given order. We also hope that this will eliminate those orders that cannot be found today by contractors. We are also introducing what I did not have time to discuss in the introduction, a very large number of conciliation and mediation proceedings. We are aware of what you were talking about when you asked us that every settlement is better and more effective than a court order. That's why we wanted to make it clear to you today that we have over 1,000. claims submitted for more than 10 billion, in disputes we have more than 10 billion PLN. Therefore, by relying on conciliation proceedings, we are launching more the Attorney General, who will be such a mediator in cases where he is not today, e.g. in matters with local government units. Of course, we also offer the possibility to mediate centres, media centres. At the same time, today in the procurement process - it is also important, which I also did not have the opportunity to discuss - the whole burden in the proceedings is primarily the choice of the contractor and the signing of the contract. In fact, the process of preparing the order, its execution, evaluation is basically omitted. In our project we place equal emphasis on the whole process, on these four elements in the procurement process, as well as on the particular importance and care, the obligations of this particular care we impose on the preparation of the order in order to eliminate often encountered situations, when we realise that we are ordering non-existent services, not to be carried out in time, and these market consultations are particularly required here and the contracting authority will have to identify itself, confirm that it has actually carried out such consultations. The question about support, Mr Meysztowicz, asked the question about how small and medium-sized companies will be able to learn about changes and learn about new regulations. To this end, we increase the role of the Public Procurement Office, we assume this support for 10 new jobs, which are primarily intended to serve, to be addressed to small and medium-sized companies. The Public Procurement Office will set up a special hotline, but it is also already preparing, developing patterns to be used for the efficient execution of orders for the contracting authorities, but also be helpful for contractors who will not be surprised by orders. So that this element is already being realized today. At the same time, the Public Procurement Office has already prepared a series of trainings with which it is ready to go to the contracting authorities as soon as the law is passed, and as I said, the long vacathio legis allows the entire market to really prepare itself well for this law. I think I've explained all your doubts. Once again, referring to time and understanding your concern... It was one of the basic charges, so thank you. I understand that there is therefore not much of these substantive allegations. However, once again, in the discussions with experts we have talked to in recent months, most often there have been voices that we cannot wait any longer, both the market and the contracting authorities are waiting for this right. The number of orders carried out, the low levels of competitiveness, leads us to convince you to use this good time of the last seymous months, so that, however, we decide to adopt this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The resolution of the Polish Sejm on commemorating the 30th anniversary of the death of Kazimierz Sabbat. 30 years ago, on July 19, 1989, Kazimierz Sabbat, immigration politician, scout and social activist, President of the Republic of Poland in exile in 1986 and 1989, died. Kazimierz Sabbat served briefly in the Navy in France during World War II and was then assigned to the brigade General Stanisław Maczek, with whom he reached the UK. As vice-president of the Scout General Committee for the War and youth referee at the General Staff of the Chief Leader in London, he led the scouting work. After the end of the war, he remained in exile, engaging in the activities of numerous immigration organizations. He co-created structures to continue his scouting work outside Poland. During his presidency there is a revival of contacts with opposition activists in his homeland. Between 1967 and 1972 he was the President of the National Unification Execution, a member of the National Council in 1972 and 1976. From 1976 to 1986 he served as Prime Minister of the Polish Government in exile. He tried to unite emigration around common goals: independence, rejection of Yalta, representation of Polish interests in the West and helping the country. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland on the 30th anniversary of the death of Kazimierz Sabbata reminds and commends his outstanding merits for his homeland. I ask the High Sejm to adopt a resolution by acclamation. [[Applause]] Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u298	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Thank you so much for that question. Anyone who can't afford to pay his debt today falls into the hands of a bailiff, often flees into a grey zone, flees with debts abroad, and the bailiff rips off his proverbial shirt, while the creditors still don't get that money. People who are spiraling into debt, not because of their own fault, often because of illness, have outbid their financial opportunities, often because of random events, have no chance of a second chance today. Therefore, consumer bankruptcy is a chance for a new life. Consumer failure is a very important project that is addressed to all those who are in insolvency. And this is the first major change, Mr President. Namely, we want the state of insolvency to be a sufficient basis for opening insolvency proceedings without investigating the reasons for the insolvency. As far as the causes are concerned, they can be various, it is a very complex matter. It is often necessary to listen to creditors. This is why these causes will be examined at a later stage. Those who, due to various random events, have not fallen into debt because of their own fault, will have a repayment period of up to 3 years. Those who, perhaps recklessly, perhaps careless, have incurred several, several debts or, for example,. outbid their financial capacity, they will have a repayment period extended to 7 years. This is the first major change. [[Voice from the room: Okay, we know.]] So the insolvency itself will be the basis for opening insolvency proceedings, which will result in almost 30,000. Poles will be able to use this system. So that we are getting closer, Mr, to France, to Britain. [[This is the third reading. Third reading.]] The second very important change. Today, however, he is fleeing into the grey zone, traveling abroad, and the creditors will not receive this money anyway. The good news is also for active debtors. If someone has a property, a house, an apartment, they will sell such property, from which they will be able to pay 70% debt? The repayment period is reduced to one year. If someone pays 50% debt as a result of voluntary repayment of the house or apartment, the repayment period will be up to 2 years. Another good news: a deal without bankruptcy. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u340	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal! Dear Mr Fellowship, Dear Members, First of all, I would like to assure you that the Ministry of Infrastructure is taking continuous action to improve the communication accessibility of every region of our country. In particular, with a view to sustainable, responsible development, we are taking consistent action that leads to a progressive improvement in the technical state of the road network. During this term - I will refer directly to the district of Gorlicka - seven tasks have been carried out on the network of national roads with the contribution of the state budget and the finances of the National Road Fund with a total amount of nearly PLN 20 million. We are convinced that these investments have a significant impact on the use of the road network in the Gorlice district. Their scope included the reconstruction of the intersection of the national road No 28 with the district road in the town of Ropa, secondly, the construction of pavements with a length of more than 400 m along the national road No 28 in Szymbark and over 700 m in Libusza, thirdly the construction of the bus bay in Gorlice, fourthly the construction of bridges over the Bielanka creek in Szymbark, on the Rodna Kąty creek in the town of Ropa and on the Ropa River in Klenczany. All further needs for the development of road infrastructure will be identified and analysed on an ongoing basis, taking into account objective criteria and implemented where financial resources are available. At the same time, the need to expand the existing network of national roads in Poland and the need to adapt infrastructure to European standards also led to the implementation of the so-called. Sądeczanki, i.e. the national road No 75 Brzesko - Nowy Sącz, and the extension of the national road No 73 on the section Pilzno - Jasło, as well as the construction of the ring road Pilzna, Brzostka, Kołaczyc and Jasła. As regards the national road No 75, work is being carried out on establishing the route in difficult mountainous areas. This investment is part of the 2014-2023 National Road Construction Programme and is now considered as a priority for preparatory work. The work in the area of the courtesan has met with great interest in local communities. The Ministry, together with the General Directorate for National Roads and Motorways and the contractor of documentation, seeks to find compromise solutions that will satisfy all interested parties. In the light of the ongoing work, it should be assumed that the implementation of this important investment for the Court of Justice would be possible between 2024 and 2027, with an estimate that the earliest section, i.e. around 2024, will be built at Brzesk. As for the national road No 73, it is part of the country's basic communication system and constitutes a very important transport route to the motorway A4 through Pilzno, Jasło and further through the national road No 28 Krosno - Sanok, followed by the national road No 84 Urzzyki Dolne up to the border crossing with Ukraine. Ladies and gentlemen! I would also like to stress that our actions focus not only on the implementation of tasks on national roads. We are aware that local authorities also have an impact on transport accessibility and communication accessibility, where funding is available under different mechanisms. In 2016 and 2018 the district task was carried out in Gorlicki County under the municipal and district road infrastructure development programme, with nearly PLN 1.5 million being co-financed and five municipal tasks with over PLN 5 million being co-financed, so that the total value of the co-financing under these investments amounts to PLN 6658 thousand. zł. In 2019, i.e. current, the Self-Government Road Fund has two municipal tasks on the list of tasks approved for co-financing, for which the amount of co-financing is 2,200 thousand. zł. And another tool, which is at the disposal of the Minister of Infrastructure and which is willingly used by local authorities, is a reserve of general subsidies. Only this year, under this tool, the local government received 1244 thousand. PLN as a co-financing for the extension of the district road no. 1506K Jankowa - Stróżna - Szalowa together with the construction of the bridge over the Stóżanka stream in Stróżna. If there are additional questions, of course the Ministry will answer. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u348	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Over recent years, the number of users of electric scooters has increased several times in Poland, which are developing relatively high speed, sometimes even within 25 km/h, using traffic. The problem of their occurrence is reflected when they are affected or when their users use an unauthorised zone or zone of residence. In the event of such a collision, however, there is a risk that the insurer may refuse to pay compensation due to the lack of regulation. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u349	Thank you very much. The answer will be given by the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Infrastructure, Minister Rafał Weber. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u350	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In fact, the topic of electric scooters is not only in the last days, weeks, but one can say that since spring very bearable. We see a great interest in this topic of residents of cities where electric scooters can be rented. We see a great use of the offer of this equipment, but we also see problems that are born on Polish sidewalks, on Polish streets, on Polish roads. Ladies and gentlemen! In recent months, new road users have appeared. So far it has been basically that road users were both vehicles: cars, motorcycles, bicycles, as well as pedestrians. For several months in larger cities, in Warsaw, in Silesia, in those cities which you mentioned, but also in Wrocław, in Poznań, there were also users of electric scooters who moved on road infrastructure. Ladies and gentlemen! As an infrastructure resort, we have prepared changes to legislation that include the rights and obligations of those who use electric scooters. The fundamental principle that we want to introduce is that the user of the personal transport device - that is what we called it - will have to use the bicycle infrastructure, i.e. bike paths, bicycle tracks, bike routes. If we adopt this law, and I hope that this will happen relatively soon, these users will be required to move just after the infrastructure for bicycles at a speed of not more than 25. We have written in the bill that this speed will be limited by design, i.e. all scooters, all personal transport devices, which will be available on the market both for sale and rental, will have to have such a structural limitation. When the road infrastructure does not have bicycle paths, such a user will be able to use the roads, but only if the maximum permitted speed on the road is 30 km/h. We believe that when a person using a scooter moves alongside a driving car that can travel at a speed higher than 30 km/h, it is too dangerous for anyone who uses an electric scooter. We believe that we should not allow such situations. When there is no cycle path, there will be no road on which the UTO user can move, there remains a pavement. We allow the use of pavement infrastructure by users of personal transport, i.e. users of electric scooters, but subject to compliance with the existing rule of law, i.e. art. Article 33 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Law on Road Traffic. This article now applies to cyclists, which is how a rider should behave when he is driving on the sidewalk. First of all, he has to watch out for the pedestrian, he has to adjust his driving, his behavior to the pedestrians. It is the pedestrians who have priority on the sidewalks, the pedestrians must feel safe on them, and anyone who is not a pedestrian on the sidewalk must adapt to those vulnerable road users. Another condition that needs to be met for personal transport equipment to be used is, according to our assumptions, the age of 10 years. A user aged 10 to 18 will have to hold a bike card. This is another example of how we copy bike solutions. Ladies and gentlemen, it is not so that at this moment only Poland has not yet settled this issue. So far, only six European countries have introduced such legislation. We already have signals that they will be corrected after a short period of time. On the other hand, we did this job for a few weeks to prepare a good project. The project was prepared and sent as a government project to the Chancellery of the President of the Council of Ministers. We look forward to including him in the list of legislative work. We hope that this draft law, as a government project, will be quickly processed by the Council of Ministers, will enter the Sejm and we will adopt this law together, because security requires it. not only users of electric scooters, but also pedestrians, cyclists and those who move motorcycles and cars. Thank you very much.

At that time, the reform - started in the middle school in 2009 and in the high school of general education in 2012 - led to the fact that unfortunately they began to disappear from the high school of general education such subjects as history, biology, physics, geography, chemistry and also workshops. Because they were only in the first class and connected so-called. III and IV educational stages. This also reflected on other general education subjects that were taught. These reforms, which were also introduced at that time, are, for example, the school obligation of six-year-olds who introduced two double yearbooks into primary school. [[I'm asking for qualifications.]] Since the deformity was established, I also tried to answer that. However, due to the reform of the education system, no qualification requirements for teachers have been changed, in other words, the statement of changes is not justified. In 2017, a new regulation was issued by the Minister of National Education on the specific qualifications required of teachers, which defined qualifications for new types of schools introduced into the system but did not change the qualification requirements. In the case of schools and special institutions, changes in the required qualifications took place in 2002, which was a very long time ago, and consisted in the fact that schools and special institutions were divided into two groups. The first group included schools and special facilities for children and young people with disabilities other than intellectual disability to a significant, deep and moderate degree, which pursue the programme basis for general education. This applies to children and adolescents with hearing impairment, visual impairment and light intellectual disability. Teachers employed in these schools and institutions are required to be qualified to teach the subject and additionally to be qualified in special education in the field of a particular disability, i.e. oligofrenopedagogics, surdopedagogics, typflopedagogics, therapeutic pedagogy. The second group of schools and institutions are those for children and young people with intellectual disabilities to a moderate, significant and deep degree and combined disabilities. Teachers in these schools and special institutions are required to be qualified in oligofrenopedagogy. The above amendment took place, as mentioned, in 2002. These rules have not changed to date. Thus, teachers who have completed their Master's degree in oligofrenopedagogics are qualified to take up teaching positions in special kindergartens, schools and special institutions for children and young people with moderate, significant and deep intellectual disabilities as well as combined disabilities, as well as in special kindergartens, special schools and special institutions for children and young people with light intellectual disabilities, are involved in conducting educational and re-educational activities. Qualification for teaching the subject of nature. Currently, the nature object is realized in class IV of 8-year-old primary school, while in addition there are biology, geography, chemistry and physics subjects and are carried out respectively in classes V, VI, VII, VIII, i.e. in VII, VIII is physics and chemistry, while geography and biology begin earlier. In accordance with the provisions, a natural teacher's position at primary school may be taken by a person who has completed, as appropriate, a Bachelor's or Master's degree in the field of specialties in accordance with the subject taught; he has completed undergraduate or Master's degree in a course whose learning outcomes referred to in the Act of 22 December 2015 on ZSK, as regards knowledge and skills, include the content of the subject or activities taught in the curriculum for the subject at an appropriate educational stage, and has a pedagogical preparation; he has completed a Bachelor's or Master's degree in another course, speciality and postgraduate studies in the subject taught; she has completed a teacher's training establishment with a specialty corresponding to the subject. As regards the regulations for the completion of post-graduate studies, it should be noted that the vast majority of primary school teachers have acquired qualifications to teach nature by completing post-graduate studies. In view of the legal regulations that have been in force since 2003 regarding teacher education standards, post-graduate programmes in the field of preparation of the subject should be developed by universities taking into account the preparation of the participants. They were most often implemented in the case of teachers of biology, physics, geography and chemistry. Teachers of these subjects who have acquired such qualifications can, of course, carry out such activities. As regards the certificates of postgraduate studies in biology, chemistry, physics and geography for which university graduates have acquired qualifications to teach middle school, the structural and programme reform of the education system has assigned this to the current 8th grade primary school. Thus, if postgraduate studies have prepared to teach in middle school, they now also confirm their qualifications to teach in primary school.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u387	Thank you very much, Marshal! Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Ladies and gentlemen! (PL) Mr President, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, Thank you very much for the work that you have done so far. You belong to those Members who are permanent guests at the Ministry of the Environment, informing us about issues related to these threats. I also had the honour of being with you immediately after the fire that took place in Zgierz. A spectacular fire, but very important, also from the perspective of everything that happened later. As far as the answer to your question is concerned, here we are dealing with a landfill located in Zgierz in the area of the former Color Industry Departments of Boruta. Diagnosing this problem, I would like to point out in the first place that the Bouruta Industry plants have passed into the hands of private operators. There are three sites in the former plants: landfill of post-production waste, including hazardous waste, landfill of plasters and ashes and so-called landfills. landfill behind Bzura, which is not a landfill within the meaning of the Waste Act. The properties on which landfills are situated are the property of the State Treasury and remain in perpetual use of Eko-Boruta Sp. a.k.a., which among others. abandoned the remediation of the landfill of post-production waste. Large amounts of waste of unknown composition and origin have been deposited on the site of these landfills. The landfill of post-production waste, including hazardous waste, requires technical intervention to stop its progressive degradation. In particular, the northern slope of the landfill slides, reveals the barrels and, as the Member has pointed out, the fluid from the trench is spilled to the ground. As far as our actions so far have been concerned, regular inspections have taken place in recent years of ash landfills, plasters and post-production waste landfills by the Environmental Protection Inspection, both during the reception of waste to the landfill and during the rehabilitation phase, combined with the carrying out of studies to assess the environmental impact. Here we are talking about hazardous waste and non-dangerous waste. In view of the analysis of the results, in particular of post-production waste, which indicates that pollution is exceeded, environmental authorities have acted in accordance with the competence of Eko-Boruta, including notification to the prosecution. As far as further action is concerned, close supervision is carried out by the Provincial Environmental Protection Supervisor. Research is being carried out to assess the state of the environment, because we still need to monitor this area due to the proximity of the river Bzura, groundwater, soil so as to have full knowledge. The cliff behind Bzura is also monitored, and it is also the edge of the river trough, which is extremely important. We also observe the state of vegetation, which is also an indicator of the functioning of the environment. The results of the IIA audit indicate the need to start the remediation of ash and plaster landfills as soon as possible and the landfill of hazardous and non- hazardous after-production waste, as well as the landfills behind Bzura, and to remove from the landfill those waste which should not be deposited on it and to carry out the collapse of the northern storage site of post-production waste. There is currently a substantial landfill of waste, which has resulted in the spilling of these effluents. As a result, the likelihood of increased concentrations of pollutants in piesometers is increasing, as we also see such pollutants in them. It is therefore necessary to isolate the areas of these two landfills from the landfill and from the impact to the rainwater. It's also about reducing the amount of leaks. The waste dump has bottom isolation and drainage, the others unfortunately do not have, it can be said that it was a kind of wild landfill. One possible way to solve the problem with the landfill behind Bzura is to use the current provisions of the Environmental Law and recognise this area as historical pollution or as an area affected by historical pollution. At the beginning of this year the Marshal of the Łódź Voivodship submitted an application to the Old Town of Zgierz. The Starosta must identify the site as a potential historical pollution of the earth's surface and enter it in the historical pollution register. This will make it possible to transfer the case to WDOŚ in Łódź. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u419	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! (PL) Mr President, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, As you have noticed, in Poland there are more than 2.5 thousand. municipal centres of culture. This is, of course, a network scattered throughout our country and is located in regions that are vulnerable to particular digital exclusion. Using the potential of municipal cultural centres, which are by nature very closely related to local communities, will effectively implement the programme's objectives, better adapting the offer to the specific needs of the site. We all know very well that those of you who function or have had the opportunity to function in local communities, small communities, that such centres are very often very active and are one of the main meeting places, especially in smaller municipalities, and at the same time they have been very often overlooked with similar actions to date. The Ministry of Digitalization decided to use this potential in the wake of the announcement of President Jarosław Kaczyński in Przydrye in 2017, which pointed to the need for digitalisation. We have decided to prepare a programme for the digitisation of municipal cultural centres. We announced this program in May this year, it has three pillars. The first is to connect all local cultural centres in Poland to broadband Internet. The second is the allocation of PLN 100 million for a cycle of digital skills training. The third is to equip the centres with modern equipment, which will remain there after the completion of the project. In the first stage, i.e. By mid-2020, i.e. 450 local cultural centres will be connected to broadband over the next year and the Ministry of Digitalisation has signed agreements with the first seven telecom operators. Other municipal cultural centres which do not have broadband internet access will be covered as a result of interventions carried out from the Operational Programme of Poland digital. Parallel, as I said, the Ministry of Digital Affairs has prepared a competition to raise digital competences, for which it has allocated PLN 100 million from the Polish digital programme, and as part of this action NGOs will be able to apply for funding in 73 competition areas of our country. Within this programme, the centres will be equipped with appropriate computer equipment, tablets, robots, educational mats and will allow, in particular, underinvested municipal centres of culture, to significantly make their training offer more attractive. In the trainings that I have already mentioned to you, we will educate not only local communities but also the employees of municipal cultural centres so that, after the course of financial training with EU funds, they can pass on the acquired knowledge. Upon completion of the training, employees will continue to receive support in the form of mentoring and up-to-date educational materials. In our view, this is extremely important as this will enable us to provide services on a permanent basis. We want to train children up to the age of 10. This will be more of a basic digital competence. Older people from 10 to 18 years of age will learn more complex competences, among others. programming lessons. We estimate that the training will be covered by 50,000. people in our country in the first stage, because then of course it will escalate. The selection of the competition for the fitting of the GOKs and for the trainings that I have told you about is planned at the end of this year. The competition has already been announced. At this point, applicants are preparing their applications, which will soon be able to evaluate the Digital Poland Project Centre. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u483	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The project against usury was initiated as a result of the observation of the prosecutor's practice. Namely, already in April 2016 in the so-called case. A large procedure was initiated in the housing usury, and a number of decommitted preparatory proceedings were launched in 2013 and 2015. It has shown gigantic abuses, violations of the rights of people who have lost their homes, houses for loan sharks, people who have been cynically deceived, who have often signed contracts, so-called. overlordship, in a situation where the extra-interest costs were several hundred, sometimes even several thousand times more than the sums that were lent to them. I will present to you only two cases in which the Attorney General brought emergency actions. The first case concerned you, who took just over 15,000. PLN loans for cosmetic cabinet equipment, meanwhile during the year interest increased, extra-interest costs, various types of margins, commissions, fees, the sum increased to more than 1,100 thousand. zł, it amounted to 730%. This case ended with an emergency complaint brought by Minister Zbigniew Ziebra and an annulment by the Supreme Court of this unfair sentence. Therefore, the reform of the Supreme Court was needed, the introduction of this new emergency control chamber was needed, and an emergency complaint was needed so that such cases of apparent injustice could be eliminated, removed and helped by these poor people. Second thing. On 26 June, recently, a month ago, another emergency complaint was filed by Minister Zbigniew Ziebra - concerning a person who borrowed about 4,000. zł, and she had to pay back $12,000. zł. The extra-interest costs amounted to 8,000. That's about 200%. the value of the loan granted. Of course, this kind of situation is something that we deal with on a daily basis. In addition, with regard to the actions of the prosecutor's office, a total of about 100 cases throughout the country were removed. Only in the Pomerania area at the moment there are about 1500 notarial acts concerning about 1000 properties under the supervision of the prosecution. Several dozen suspects have been investigated. Nearly 300 charges were brought against them. About 1,000 victims were found. The material is collected in about 700 volumes of case files. The total value of the property lost by the victims in all of these, almost 300, amounts to over PLN 32 million. For the first time, it was applied by our government, prepared by us in the Ministry of Justice, so-called. Expanded seizures. In total, the property was secured for the amount of approx. 8 million PLN. A compulsory mortgage was established on approximately 66 properties. There's more victims coming in all the time. This wave of giant fraud, injustice, shows that weed is a huge problem in our country. In view of the above, the same bill was created, which comprehensively resolves the situation, in particular by introducing a clear, fixed limit on non-interest costs: all margins, commissions, additional charges and insurance will not be able to exceed 20%. Another very important issue is the ban on expropriation. We forbid this kind of action. The ownership of the property will be irrelevant. The idea is to protect people in a forced position, people who often, for personal reasons, are not able to cope with such deceitful, draconian rates, extra-interest costs, and thus lose their entire life's work. This type of activity will be prohibited. Another, third shift. We're introducing regulations on the crime of usury. What is now, ladies and gentlemen, is in the article. 304, that's exploitation. This is not a classic usury crime. It is therefore necessary to introduce clear, objective criteria for the liability of perpetrators of this type of crime. Current criteria, which are very arbitrary and discretionary, often allow those who harm others to escape justice. I would like to remind you that between 2009 and 2010 almost 90% cases of proceedings were either cancelled or refused to initiate them. Only 20 cases have been brought to the courts and only four have been convicted. Three, four - two in the last year - cases end in convictions each year. These numbers, these facts call for vengeance to heaven. [[Exactly.]] Therefore, this law is necessary to help people in such a crisis situation. One of the cases which ended in refusing to initiate proceedings, ladies and gentlemen, at that time, in 2008-2010, was the case of a lady who borrowed PLN 650 and paid 21,000. She's still got 52 grand to pay. zł. The prosecution refused to initiate proceedings in this case. Almost 90% the proceedings ended with a write-off or a refusal to open in the years 2008 and 2010. Now these matters are being reopened, reopened. Indictments are being directed, and the criminals are being taken away. These are the successes of a good change. [[Applause]] This involves another very important change in our project: no one can be thrown out on the pavement for several hundred PLN debt. Therefore, we propose to exclude execution from real estate in a situation where the debt is less than 5% value of real estate. Another shift. We want to introduce a solution whereby these loan companies, various types of lending institutions, will be supervised by the Financial Supervision Commission. The Financial Supervision Commission will check all these procedures, be able to impose penalties, analyse whether such infringements are occurring. The next case is related to the so-called. Rolling. Rolling based on the so-called. the compound percentage causes the debt to increase in geometric progress. We therefore propose to ban the rolling of loans. Lending companies often use these types of runways, calling them deferred payment of the loan. It's got nothing to do with the adjournment. It's to get a client unaware of his situation into a giant spiral of debt. That's why we want to stop rolling. Ladies and gentlemen! Finally, I'm going to talk about something that's important in this place. Namely, in 2011 two very important regulations came out of the Anti-Liquiary Act. It's about art. 7a and 18a of the Consumer Credit Act. The first of these provisions made it clear that the maximum amount of non-pension costs was met, and the second indicated that the sentence of imprisonment up to the age of 2 was subject to those who claimed costs higher than those laid down by law. These provisions of the Anti-Liquiary Act were thrown out under very strange circumstances, and were indicated in the explanatory memorandum, with full awareness and cynicism, I stress that consumers' interests may be harmed by the need to bear higher costs than before, because creditors will benefit from removing the maximum cost limit from the legal order and will raise fees, commissions and other costs associated with the conclusion of a consumer credit agreement, this can happen. And with full awareness it was done. The effect did not have to be waited long - in the last few months loan sharks from all over the world flew here at the expense of our citizens. Ladies and gentlemen! The changes introduced in 2015 by the Civic Platform are only apparent, they are a powder of this system and do not bring anything, as the report of the Financial Ombudsman of December ub clearly shows. year. The number of complaints to the Financial Ombudsman is constantly increasing. Last year it was 1700 complaints, in the previous year - about 1500 complaints. The financial Ombudsman clearly shows how these solutions, which were introduced at the time, can be dealt with. First of all, there is still no clear limit on non-interest costs, still insurance, margins, commissions, fees are the peril of our citizens. The financial Ombudsman even shows a graphical example of such a classic circumvention of the current law: the amount of loan received by the client in hand - 9 000. PLN, while all additional costs - 11 thousand, i.e. the maximum amount to be paid by the customer - 20 thousand. zł. This is, of course, entirely acceptable and various mechanisms to circumvent this law are clearly indicated in this report by the Financial Ombudsman. Therefore, it is our proposal to seal this system, to prohibit fraudulent methods used by various types of loaners, such toxic instruments. We take an example from other countries. In Poland the loan is only on paper, ladies and gentlemen, six times more expensive than in Germany and three and a half times more expensive than in Slovakia. I say: only on paper, because in fact these costs are 730% as in one example, 200%, 1000%. So that loan companies, all kinds of loan sharks have made themselves an eldorado in our country, preying on our citizens, preying on them. We can't let that happen. That is why I ask you to vote in favour of this bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u533	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the PSL - Coalition Poland club to present our position on the government draft law amending certain laws in order to counter usury, print no. 3600. This project aims to protect people exposed to usury and reduce the socially harmful phenomenon of usury in Poland. The project provides for a prison sentence from 3 months to 5 years for anyone who, in exchange for a natural person's cash benefit resulting, inter alia. the loan contract requires it to pay additional costs twice as much as the maximum or to require it to pay interest at a rate twice as high as the maximum. The authors also propose the introduction of supervision of the Financial Supervision Commission over loan institutions. However, the main tool of the new law is to reduce the upper limit of the non-interest-bearing cost of consumer credit. Currently, for several years now, there has been a limit of 55% off-set costs. The first version adopted in mid-June by the government of the so-called project. The Anti-Liquiary Act provided for a reduction of this limit to 45%, a week later the limit was reduced to 20% and eventually reduced to 10%. High Izbo! Without a doubt, we should effectively fight the cases of usury, including as drastic as described in the justification of the project. However, the question needs to be asked: Were these incorrect provisions the cause of these cases and whether the solutions proposed in the draft will solve this problem? The examples used by the authors of the draft law to illustrate the importance of the problem of usury and to justify the need for legislative intervention refer to cases of contracts which have been concluded long before the entry into force of the provisions already in force for several years establishing a limit on the non-remuneration costs of consumer credit or which are not subject to the provisions of the law. So it is not the current law that is the source of these damned practices. Their source is the lack of effectiveness of law enforcement and justice in enforcing existing legislation. It is also debatable that such a drastic, jumping reduction of the upper limit of the non-interest-bearing cost of consumer credit is so drastic. In our view, this should be done gradually, over the years, in order to properly assess the impact of the new rates on the situation of borrowers and lenders. Initial proposed 45% , so 10% reduction It would be a more cautious policy. We could assess the functioning of the market and the situation of customers of credit institutions under new conditions and possibly continue this trend in the future. In the current form, the proposed rules are hitting legally operating loan companies, which are already subject to the UOKiK and KNF. Today there is no shortage of opinions and analyses that say that the whole industry, employing 40,000. people, will fall after a sudden decrease in the limits of extra-interest costs. For the budget, this will mean a decrease in revenue per year by PLN 2.2 billion, because this is what the sector of loan institutions pays in taxes. An important question that is raised in the debate on the loan market is the question of the shadow economy and the illegal segment of the market, which is out of control and which often forces the recovery of claims by brutal methods. The question cannot be asked: Will not existing customers of credit institutions who charge salty fees for their services, but who are under the supervision of the State, move to such shady sources of funding? Legal lenders, if they survive on the market at all after a drastic reduction in the upper limit of non-interest-bearing credit costs, will be most likely to judge customers more strongly in terms of their ability to repay the loans and will much more often refuse them these loans. Such customers can find themselves in a much worse situation than today, desperate to use illegal sources of funding, where debt recovery is much more severe and painful, sometimes literally painful. Let us also expect the explosion of pawn shops and the grey zone, a very large pathology of social relations against the financial background. This will be a huge problem for the prosecution, which will be flooded with reports of various types of minor loan fraud, which will be out of control because the market hates vacuum. Isn't it better to pay a few dozen percent for a loan, but within the limits of state law and control, under a legal agreement with an institution under state supervision, than to fall into the hands of gray-zone lenders, i.e. real loan sharks? The project also includes changes that we welcome. It is important to commend the conclusion of the project with the principle that the pursuit of the loan shark was dependent on whether he knew that the person taking the loan was in a forced position, i.e. in a difficult situation of life, and dramatically needs money to ensure the basic conditions of life, such as food, medicines, billing fees. This discretion of law has so far been the cause of the impunity of loan sharks. In the most drastic cases, when the debtor, after taking out a loan of PLN 500.600, had to repay several tens of thousands of zlotys, this provision led to the remission of the case. It is good that the project assumes the end of freedom in setting additional charges also for loans granted under civil law agreements, i.e. borrowing. in a private person. As the practice shows, many appalling cases of usury are precisely this type of loan, not provided by lending companies, but by other entities. On the plus side, it should also be calculated that it will not be possible to request the transfer of ownership of the property to cover the liabilities incurred. The possibility to carry out bail-out will be eliminated if the amount of debt does not exceed 5% the value of the house or premises. We have already had cases in Poland where people lost their homes for several hundred zloty loans. High Izbo! The presented government project goes very far, radically changes the loan market in Poland. In its current form, with a 10% cut in off-interest costs, it can eliminate the entire industry, operating under the law and under supervision. It's an employee of $40,000. people and paying over PLN 2 billion of taxes to the state budget. In addition, which does not correspond to the most drastic cases of usury, described widely in the justification of the project. At the same time, by introducing a 10% off-interest cost limit, you will lead to the expansion of the grey zone and the associated pathology of social life. In addition to the provisions that can actually contribute to the elimination of the phenomenon of usury, the project also unfortunately contains provisions that will eliminate the entire sector of companies, companies that comply with the rules in force, have nothing to do with these pathological phenomena. We can't afford that. Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the club PSL - Coalition Poland I see the need for far-reaching changes in the project. In the proposed shape it does not lead to actual protection against usury.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u536	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! The reasons for the draft law submitted by the Council of Ministers amending the Family and Care Code Act are the need arising from the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, which was issued on 16 May 2018 in case K 18/17, and the need to amend the article. 70 § 1 Family and Care Code. The Court found that this provision was incompatible with several provisions of the Constitution: Article. 30, in conjunction with Article. 47, Art. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Constitution, in respect of which it sets a time limit for an adult child to bring an action for denial of the fatherhood of his mother's husband, regardless of the date on which that child becomes aware that it does not originate from his mother's husband. The provision provides that a child may bring such an action within 3 years of his or her age. However, such a time limit applies to a full-time child also in the case of an action for denial of motherhood, as stated in the article. 61 § 1 Family and caring code, and actions for determination of the ineffectiveness of the recognition of fatherhood governed by Article. 81 § 2 Family and caring code. There has therefore been a need to harmonise the rules and the proposal proposes to amend both provisions in the same way as in the case of the Article. 70 § 1, which was challenged by the Constitutional Tribunal. The amendment is to modify the beginning of the period for an adult child to bring an action in each of these actions by indicating that the period runs from the moment the child learns that it is not biologically derived from the person who is included in the birth certificate as a mother or a father, and that the period does not begin to run if the child has obtained such information before he or she is of age. In this case, the beginning of the run is the day that the child reaches age. In view of the progress made in determining the origin of the child and the natural pursuit of the truth in terms of biological origin, it is necessary, at least in our opinion, to extend to 1 year the current six-month time limits for each of the actions provided for in different situations, inter alia. Article 2 61 § 1 and 2, Art. 63, Art. 64 § 1, Art. 64 (2), Art. 65, Art. 69 § 1, Art. 78 § 1 in matters for recognition and as a consequence in Article. 76 § 2 Family and caring code. It's about accepting the fatherhood of a dead child. Based on the considerations made in the statement of reasons made by the Constitutional Tribunal, the draft also concluded that the mother's husband should be able to bring an action for denial of fatherhood within a time limit calculated from the moment he became aware that he was not the father of his wife's child and not from the moment he was informed of the birth of the child by his wife. It is now mentioned in the article. 63 and Article 63 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Similarly, the mother should be able to bring an action for denying her husband's father's father from the moment she realized that he was not the father of her child, not since the child was born, as is currently regulated by the article. 69 § 1 of the Family and Care Code. For this reason, we are updating the rules accordingly, and we are proposing to amend them. In the interests of systemic consistency, a limitation will be introduced that the bringing of such an action may take place at the latest by the date on which the child reaches his/her age. Given that time limits are extended for the mother and father to bring negative actions in relation to the origin of the child and that the time limit for such action by the child is to start running from the moment the child becomes aware that it does not originate from the person concerned, it is foreseen that the time limit for the child should also be one year. Only during a transitional period, in order not to deprive the child of entitlement in this respect, a period of 3 years from the date of entry into force of the Act was adopted. Of course, it is also desirable that the proceedings for the denial of fatherhood or for the denial of motherhood and for the determination of the ineffectiveness of the recognition of fatherhood initiated before the date of entry into force of the Act be completed on the basis of provisions which are more favourable to the parties. Therefore, the principle of applying new rules in these proceedings should be adopted, and in this respect the Article is being designed. 2 (2) 1 amendment. The proposed law should enter into force 30 days after the date of publication. This is a short time, given that it is a matter of regulation of the Code, but the proposed changes do not complicate existing legal relations, do not diminish the powers of the persons concerned, so there is no obstacle to such a deadline. The draft submitted is the continuation of those changes in the Family and Care Code which were initiated on the basis of a law adopted unanimously by Parliament of 16 May 2019 amending the Family and Care Code and the Civil Procedure Act. Please accept the submitted proposal. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u537	Thank you, Minister. I would like to ask Senator Alexander Swede to present the justification for the bill contained in print 3486.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-19-sejm-84-3.u538	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Senate of the Republic of Poland an initiative on amending the law Family and caring code. This amendment is the implementation of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. The Constitutional Tribunal in its judgment of 16 May 2018 e.g. Act SK 18/17 found non-compliance with Article. 70 § 1 of the Law of 25 February 1964. The Family and Care Code with the Constitution. The Court held that that provision, in so far as it sets a time limit for an action against the fatherhood of a mother's husband, irrespective of the date on which an adult child becomes aware of the fact that it does not originate from a mother's husband, is incompatible with the Constitution. The problem is that this term is completely disconnected from the moment when the person concerned learned that it was not from his mother's husband. The legal solution provided for in the contested Article. 70 § 1, which closes the investigation of the denial of fatherhood after the expiry of the 3-year deadline, constitutes a restriction on the constitutional right to know its own biological roots. The proposed amendment proposes that a child should be able to bring an action after age to deny the fatherhood of his mother's husband, but no later than three years after his or her age. However, if the child learns that it does not come from his mother's husband after he is of age, the time limit will begin to run from the day the child learns about the circumstances. The three-year period for a child to bring an action for denial of motherhood or the determination of the ineffectiveness of the recognition of fatherhood shall be determined in the same way as in the case of denial of fatherhood. Thus, in the Senate's proposal for an amendment, it is proposed that the family and caring code of 25 February 1964 should be amended as follows. Art. 61 § 1 is replaced by the following: "A child may bring an action for denial of motherhood within three years of the attainment of adulthood. If the child has heard of the circumstances referred to in Article. 61 § 1, when the child is of age, that period shall begin to run from the date on which the child becomes aware of the circumstances. Art. 70 § 1 is replaced by the following: "A child may, after reaching adulthood, bring an action for denial of the fatherhood of his mother's husband, but no later than three years after the attainment of adult age. If the child has heard of the circumstances referred to in Article. 67, when the child is of age, that period starts to run from the day the child learns about the circumstances. Art. 81 § 2 is replaced by the following: With this request, the child may come to age, but no later than three years after it has been achieved. If the child becomes aware of the circumstances referred to in paragraph 1, the period shall begin to run from the date on which the child becomes aware of the circumstances when he or she is of age. The Act would enter into force 14 days after the date of the announcement. Joint Senate committees: the Legislative Commission and the Committee on Family, Senior and Social Policy worked on the proposed amendment on 13 February and 10 April. A number of opinions were presented during the consultation for the proposed amendment. I would like to stress that among others. The Supreme Court, the Social Insurance Institution, the Ombudsman of the Rights of the Child, the KRS, the Prosecutors' Association of Lex super omnia, the KRUS and the Supreme Bar Council have not submitted any comments, while the Prosecutor General and the Ombudsman have submitted comments on the article. 60, taking into account the reference to Article. 67. These comments have been taken into account in this amendment. The Ministry of Justice also expressed its opinion, whose similar project, although in a slightly extended version, is also subject to the procedure at this point. This Senate initiative on amending the Family and Care Code was adopted at the 78th Senate meeting on 8 May this year. by 83 votes to one abstention. This regulation will allow legal confirmation of biological identity and will adapt the system of right to the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. Therefore, on behalf of the Senate of the Republic of Poland, I request that the Sejm adopt the attached draft amendment to the Family and Care Code Act in print No 3486 or that the proposals submitted be taken into account in further work on the joint project within the framework of a broader proposal prepared by the government. Thank you very much.
